ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u1	Ladies and gentlemen, take the seats. The meeting was resumed. We continue with the debate on the opinion of the draft Law on General State Budgets for 2018. Firstly, the debate on Section 20, Energy, Tourism and the Digital Agenda. For the presentation of the section, the Minister for Energy, Tourism and Digital Agenda has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u2	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I appear today to defend the general lines of the draft Law on General State Budgets for 2018 corresponding to section 20, Ministry of Energy, Tourism and Digital Agenda. For 2018, the ministry's budget amounts to €5599.65 million, an increase of just over 6% over the 2017 budget. The non-financial part of the budget, i.e. that which affects the deficit, is EUR 4538 million, which represents a slight increase over last year - slightly less than 1% - and the financial budget has an additional EUR 300 million, which represents a substantial increase of 39.42 %. In other words, financial transactions are, by contrast, the ones that go up the most in this section. Chapters 1, staff costs, and 2, current expenditure on goods and services, show a slight increase, with 57 million and 17 million respectively. [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u3	Silence, ladies and gentlemen, please; silence. Go on.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u4	The total allocation for current and capital transfers, the most important part of the ministry, especially that affecting the energy sector, amounts to €4414 million. The main provisions of these headings of Chapters 4 and 7 include compensation to undertakings for the cost of using electricity from renewable sources. This is an item that already existed in the previous budget and which appears for the second time, in line with a procedure that is being used by several Member States of the European Union, what is called avoiding carbon leakage, and is endowed with 150 million euros.Then there is an allocation of 8.5 million for the potabilizers of the Canary Islands and 3 million more than last year, in addition to the credits worth 67.2 million euros to IDAE, one of the most important institutes within the ministry, which is responsible for energy efficiency and renewable energy. Real investments are very small within the Ministry of Energy, Tourism and Digital Agenda, they are barely 50 million euros, and affect the functioning of the ministry's own services. And, as I said earlier, Chapter 8 is experiencing a substantial increase of EUR 300 million, especially in the increase in endowment for the Institute for the Development of Alternative Energy. For areas of expenditure, I will start with the Energy State Secretariat, with which the budget responds to the main priorities of the government's energy policy. These priorities are, as you well know: to ensure a secure and competitive energy supply; to bet on improving energy efficiency; to introduce renewable energy in an efficient and sustainable way; the interconnection of the Spanish energy system with the rest of the European Union; and the framework for the Government's commitment to the environmental objectives set by the European Union. The energy budget amounts to €4.5 billion - slightly more - and represents an increase of 314 million compared to 2017. This increase illustrates the importance we are attaching to energy efficiency and renewable energy policy. There are three basic types of action for this purpose. First, the financing of the electrical system. As you know, part of the tariff deficit has been covered by contributions from the ministry to avoid further increases in the receipt of light. Secondly, support for the economic and social fabric linked to coal. And, third, energy efficiency and renewable energy. The financing of the electricity system represents the fundamental amount of the ministry, 3750 million, and its objective, as I said before, is to balance the tariff deficit, a balance that was achieved for the first time in 2014 and that has been maintained - even with some surplus - in 2015, 2016 and 2017. It is the first time for a long time that in Spain we have a balance in the system and this has allowed to freeze or even reduce for the fifth consecutive year the tolls of access to the system, which are directly impacted on the fixed part of the receipt, what is called the term of power. That is to say, thanks to the fact that we have a tariff balance, the fixed part of the receipt has been kept constant or even reduced for five years, and in part there is a public contribution to this situation, which comes from this budget line of the ministry. Support for the sector-related social and economic fabric is the implementation of the coal mining policy framework and the promotion of the economic development of the mining regions. Why? Because, as we all know, in 2010 a European regulation was established, a decision by the Commission and the Council, which marked the path of conversion of the sector, a tremendously difficult path of conversion and which has affected more than 1600 workers, so that we have barely 1000 workers in public mining and about 300 in private mining, of a staff that came to be in 2010 over 6000 people. This very important conversion has given rise to all kinds of social aid, both to people and to the regions, and in these we have pre-retirement and reduced incentives of 230 million euros and a whole series of actions both for the environmental restoration of mines and for the creation of business incentives and measures to support the infrastructures of the regions. Furthermore, as a third fundamental element, we support renewable energy and energy efficiency. The most important item is the National Energy Efficiency Fund, apart from the Feder funding of certain items that are complemented by the budget. Among the main actions we have the second call of the Plan of incentives for the rehabilitation of buildings; the Plan Grows II, with 204 million euros, and the programme of incentives to carry out actions of energy efficiency, with 95.2 million euros. In addition, we have a new budget of EUR 300 million of financial contributions from the system itself to facilitate the implementation of structural funds. This is a scheme that is also applied in the Digital Agenda, where funds are advanced by the system, projects are implemented, Community funds are collected and once these funds are collected they can be re-advanced, so that the project implementation capacity is greatly accelerated. Also, in the same bill of the General Budgets of the State for 2018 we have actions to support the investment of electrical production facilities from renewable sources charged to the electrical surplus for a maximum amount of 60 million euros. This will enable renewable energy auctions to be carried out more quickly, as was the case last year. Finally, for the first time there is a transfer of 2 million euros from IDAE to the Bimep research centre. As far as the Information Society and the Digital Agenda are concerned, as you know, the main objective is the digitisation of the Spanish economy and its society and the creation of the conditions that will enable the improvement of the competitiveness of companies and social welfare. The total budget of the Secretariat of State is €670 million, of which 126 is a non-financial budget and 544 is a financial budget. It is important to continue to encourage the deployment of state-of-the-art broadband throughout the territory. Spain already has a coverage of 71% fiber optics and 76% fixed high speed networks and the idea is to continue to progress. Spain is the country of Europe that has the most fiber optics deployed, it has more than the sum of the United Kingdom, Italy and Germany together. And following, advancing in this very wake to get Spain to have the best fiber network and the best access by citizens to networks of the latest generation, we have launched the Plan 300x100, which is equipped for the next years of 525 million euros, 150 for the call of 2018. In addition, we have the development of 5G as the latest wireless technology. The 5G has several elements of development, one of them are the pilot projects, the first places where 5G will be installed with cases of use, how you can use a technology that expands the bandwidth and virtually eliminates latency, which have a budget of 20 million euros. Apart from this call, 24 million euros are envisaged for Networks transfers for the National Plan of Smart Territories, which will also involve digital developments of all kinds and which will have to do with both the 5G, the development of new networks as well as especially with new applications - that is what matters -; that is, on those networks what elements we can do, that you know that you have all kinds of thematic concentrations, especially energy, rural and depopulation, and also tourism. There will also be €4.5 million for actions to support the development of digital skills. This complements the work being done by the Ministry of Employment in this area. And then we have additional resources for the technical support offices of the Spanish Network of Smart Cities and for the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces because it is very important that at the local level the digitization process has support from the State. Like every year, €5 million will go to the Mobile World Capital Barcelona Foundation. It has not been easy to keep Mobile in Barcelona, it has been a great effort and the explanations and guarantees that we have had to give from the Government of Spain have been very bold, but it is also true that other administrations have been very helpful in this. Of course, we have guaranteed the smooth operation of Mobile as one of the most important technological events of the year not only at European level but at global level. Of course, in this budget is the contribution that the State makes to the patronage. We have 23 million euros for Incibe - we are talking about cybersecurity -, with this we make a major effort to invest in cybersecurity for all citizens. Incibe is the part of the public administration that is dedicated to the protection of citizens and businesses; in other ministerial areas critical infrastructures are found and other types of attacks are received, but here we are talking about what most affects all users. Of course the work that Incibe is doing deserves this budgetary contribution that we are doing. To complete these actions, we have two initiatives that fall within the overall capacities of the Secretariat of State: the Group of Wise Men on Artificial Intelligence and Big Data, which we are going to launch, and the working table to address the gender gap in technology. In terms of tourism, the Ministry's budget is 316 million, an increase of 13 million over the previous year, 4.58 per cent. Bearing in mind that it is a competence that in 100 per cent of the autonomous statutes is exclusive, we are talking about the residual competences left to the ministry as the General Administration of the State, which essentially relate to promotion abroad and to generating areas of coordination between the different autonomous communities. As you know, tourism activity last year was successful. The number of international tourists received amounts to almost 82 million, surpassing the United States and only behind France. In tourism spending we are only behind the United States; international tourists spent 86 billion euros in Spain. The World Economic Forum has already described Spain on different occasions in recent years as the most competitive tourist destination in the world. The prospects for 2018 are good, with the idea of continuing on the path of growth. In the first three months of this year, which are accounted for, we have an increase of 6% of the figure, so the outlook is moderate with respect to the increases in previous years, but in terms of historical peaks we continue to grow. That is why it is necessary to continue with our work of promoting tourism and, above all, of promoting the new markets; that is, of the tourist origins that are not so common, because you know that the vast majority of tourists coming to our country come from the European Union, especially from the United Kingdom, Germany and France, but there are also more distant destinations and that they are having a strong tourist potential, and I am talking about Russia and Asia, especially China. And on the other hand is the enhancement of the tourist product not so mature, not so well known as the sun and beach, the so-called indoor tourism, which encompasses a lot of products: nature tourism, sports tourism, cultural tourism, gastronomic tourism, urban tourism, shopping tourism. It is a medium-high segment with high added value and is having increments every year to two digits and generating a tourist development in autonomous communities that did not have so much tourist tradition and that, however, is doing them extraordinarily well in this respect. The main games in this type of promotion, with these ideas that I have just commented on are: the support for the internationalization of tourism companies, with 74 million euros; the Emprendatur project - the R & D+i projects in the field of tourism -, with 78 million euros, and then we have a whole series of games dedicated to the rehabilitation of infrastructure of equipment in mature tourist areas, those destinations that started in the sixties and that need support because they are the best areas - they are those in which the tourist activity began - but often have obsolete infrastructures. There are 18.5 million euros for this type of action, 4 million more than in the previous year. As I said, we have a special impact on strengthening tourism products, on supporting the new products I mentioned earlier and also on the new markets. And special relevance will have the tourist support that we will give through Turespana to the areas affected by last year's attacks in August, which were precisely Barcelona and Cambrils, the whole of Catalonia, which in addition to the political events of autumn also suffered a very deteriorated tourist situation, which fortunately has recovered with the political stability that was won with 155 and at the time when the campaigns of tourist promotion will be made a special impact to recover that image that in its day was lost from the first autonomous community in terms of tourism of Spain, which is precisely Catalonia. [[Rumors]] We will also have a specific match for tourism promotion in the UK as a result of the Brexit. We enter the second year of the Brexit and we need to make a special effort to inform our British customers that Spain will have exactly the same conditions - or as similar as possible - as they have been enjoying as the first country of origin of tourists. That is why, as I said before, within the scope of the State's action, we are specifically focusing on areas where there may have been some kind of problem or difficulty in the tourism sector, in addition to the general promotion of the most innovative products and which have the most capacity for growth in our country. Finally, I would like to highlight the momentum being given by the Government to the Tourism Paradores Network. An additional €18 million is planned: 14 for construction works, you know that the ministry is responsible for the infrastructure, i.e. the renovation of the Paradores because in addition the vast majority of them are listed buildings in all types of protection, the vast majority of them in the maximum. And besides, an additional $4 million for rehabilitation. That is, construction and rehabilitation. [[The Rumors Continue]]

Ladies and gentlemen, in a serious country the decision to close a power plant dedicated to electricity production cannot be taken from behind the Government and against the general interest. The rule of law must ensure the supply of electricity at risk in its prices, in competition, in the electricity market or in the fulfilment of the environmental objectives arising from the closure of a thermal power plant. This competence must be attributed to a regulated decision of the Government, not to the whimsical decision of any enterprise, whether public or private. The closure of coal power plants destroys the Spanish industrial fabric, further increases the price of electricity, with very negative effects on the economy of our families and on the competitiveness of our companies and industries, and opens the door from behind to the allocation of longer hours of operation to nuclear power plants. Ladies and gentlemen, a large number of amendments tabled to this section by the opposition groups refer to the Tax Agency and the resources allocated to the fight against tax fraud. The fight against fraud and tax evasion remains one of the priority objectives of the People's Party Government and the figures contained in these budgets reflect this. I would like to highlight the significant increase of more than EUR 28 million envisaged in the initial budget allocation of the Tax Agency. The upward trend in the tax agency's budgets over the previous four years therefore continues. The Tax Agency also has a credit-generation mechanism that will provide an additional EUR 303 million, resulting in a final budget of EUR 1392 million. This represents an increase of 8.03 per cent over the 2017 budget execution. With regard to human resources, which are insufficient, it is estimated that by 31 December 2018 the staffing of the Tax Agency will be 25,555, with a net increase of 396 more. Ladies and gentlemen, the policy is measured by the results achieved, and the results in the fight against fraud in recent years, especially from 2015 to 2017, are historic. In the period 2013-2017, the agency's actions have led to collecting results in the prevention and fight against fraud of more than 68,600 million euros, which shows the efficiency and effectiveness of the Tax Agency, of its professionals, who deserve our recognition because they work with dedication and effort for Spanish society. The Tax Agency has better legal tools, better computer tools and more and better persecutors. For all these reasons, the People's Parliamentary Group is going to vote against the amendments tabled to section 15 and we call on the opposition groups to act responsibly in support of these budgets. These budgets, ladies and gentlemen, are the guarantee that in 2018 there will be 500 000 new jobs, are the guarantee that Spain will continue to lead Europe in growth and job creation and that we can move together towards that great goal that I believe we all share here: to get 20 million Spaniards working in 2020. ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u62	Thank you very much, Mrs Madrazo. The next item is the debate on section 14, Defence. For the section presentation, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister of Defence.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u63	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear before this House to set out the content of the draft State General Budgets Act relating to national defence, which is to be provided in the current financial year with appropriations amounting to EUR 8455.9 million. The budget of section 14 grows by 817.4 million, 10.70% compared to 2017. However, in spite of this growth and in order to improve the comparison with the previous year by making homogeneous comparisons, we must bear in mind that EUR 314 million is part of one of the biggest developments in the accounts that I present to you today and which also addresses issues and requests that we have discussed on many occasions in this House, I am referring to the initial budgetary allocation of peacekeeping operations. Peacekeeping operations, regardless of the final cost each year, were usually provided with only a small amount in the budget proposal, then supplemented by budget concepts and, through budget concepts 128, 228 and 668, with contributions from the Contingency Fund. Well, we have been able to include a little over 300 million in this exercise from the outset, which is likely to make it difficult to compare directly between budget periods, but which certainly makes budgetary management and the budget allocation of missions very easy. In this way, in order to better establish the references, if we take into account the initial appropriations for the 2017 budget year and add the total disbursed in operations, we get a figure of 8701 million euros, with 1076.8 million euros in peacekeeping operations. As far as the current year is concerned, if there are no major changes in the forecast for peacekeeping operations for 2018, amounting to 1114 million euros, we hope to reach the end of the 9255.9 million euros. In other words, there is a homogeneous budget increase of EUR 554.8 million, which translates in percentage by 6.38%. [[The lady]] This increase, which doubles that experienced by our country's gross domestic product in 2017, is a clear expression of the importance the Government attaches to defence and protection policy. We have been through very difficult years in all areas and have been particularly difficult in the areas of defence and protection of Spain's interests within and outside our borders. The economic situation that we were in when we arrived, ladies and gentlemen, did not even allow the inclusion in the initial budget of inescapable payment obligations, we had to look for the money after negotiation and then incorporate it. This government and this ministry were able to do so and now, at last, in a period of new economic growth and also of employment, we can reinvigorate basic ministerial spending. We would have liked it better, but it is a fact that there is a ceiling rise of just over 1.5 billion for all basic ministerial spending, and a part of it is going to be devoted to equipping our defence policy. With what? With better means, with renewed infrastructure to improve the quality of life of our military, with new technologies and, of course, with better salaries. Since 2000 the defence had never before had a greater weight in terms of the percentage of non-financial expenditure of the State; therefore, we have finally recovered the levels of defence investment prior to the crisis. We have also provided the budget with a balance between staff costs, 54.5 per cent, and material costs, 45.5 per cent, which had been lost and which kept us away from the situation of other defence ministries and other armed forces in countries that are in our environment, particularly within the European Union. Of course, we cannot and must not give ourselves up to self-congratulation because we have taken very much into account the effects that the crisis has had on the budget of the Ministry of Defence, and the budgetary situation of national defence continues to require a sustained effort. What we are doing with this budget is pushing in the right direction. If in the previous financial year, with an increase of 0.6%, we moved very slightly, with the current budget we are taking decisive steps along the path that marks us, on the one hand, the objectives of budgetary consolidation and compliance with the deficit that Spain meets with strict observance, as well as our Community partners, and on the other hand, we are fulfilling our road map in the field of European security and defence policy, the PESCO, the fourth pillar of Community policies, as well as improving our commitments with our allies. It is a path we want to preserve because it must lead us to growth and employment, as well as to the guarantee of peace and security. In this way, our tour in 2018 will lead us to a total defence expenditure, in terms of the Atlantic Alliance, of 0.93% of GDP, taking into account that other items are also incorporated, which are the pensions of military personnel. Returning to the Ministry of Defence's budget for the current year, I will briefly quote some of the magnitudes and items that I would like to highlight. On the one hand, the budget for staff in Chapter 1 is increasing by EUR 72 million. It is a 1.6% rise that coincides with what the government and social partners have agreed on in the 2018 Public Employment Pact, which will lead to an increase in wages of more than 8% over the next three years. We thus improve the salaries of the men and women who are part of our Armed Forces, as we also improve the salaries of all public servants who are part of the Ministry and the General Administration of the State. As far as current expenditure is concerned, ladies and gentlemen, the increase is 12.33%, which is justified by several fundamental actions that we are undertaking and which coincide with work lines that have already been announced. First of all, EUR 12.31 million will be devoted to the use of the Peace satellite, launched on 22 February, with which we have acquired our own Earth observation capabilities, including radar technology, which we had never provided for. With the Peace satellite we can have real-time information images that strengthen our security and also our intelligence capabilities and place us on an equal footing in collaboration with our partners and allies. Except for Germany, Spain is the only country in our environment that has an observation system as perfect and technologically up-to-date as the Paz satellite, also a very important boost for research and technology in our country. Another figure that I would like to highlight in this chapter 2 is the increase of 13.39 million euros for the maintenance of infrastructures, 27.43% more than in 2017. This departure, ladies and gentlemen, has a vocation of continuity and comes to fulfill an objective that we had stated is to solve the deterioration, sometimes very serious, of the facilities through actions that will result in the well-being of our Armed Forces with regard to both the areas of work, barracks, and the residential endowments. The shock plan for improving the quality of life of the military being addressed by the Ministry of Defence is incardinated within this action. As far as Chapter 4 is concerned, I must stress the budgetary reflection of the importance this Government attaches to the training and training of our military, with the increases in the appropriations of the university defence centres reaching an average of 30 per cent. I close this review of chapters and items with those for Chapter 6, that of real investments. For this 2018 financial year the chapter will have an allocation of 2841.9 million euros, an increase of 651.6 million euros, that is, 29.75% regarding the previous financial year. And the increase in chapter 6 is reflected in all aspects of it. We maintain the investment and transparency exercise that involved the full incorporation of the amounts planned to deal with the special programmes and armaments, we increase by 340 million euros the amounts that are going to be allocated to the flow of repayment of the credit advances granted by the industry and we initially provide the chapter of investments of the operations, which greatly facilitates their implementation. In addition, the increase in the amount of appropriations also covers logistical support, the modernization of the Armed Forces and the research and development programmes of the Armed Forces. Improvements of more than EUR 34 million are incorporated. In short, we demonstrate with facts in this budget, ladies and gentlemen, what we seek: to provide the Armed Forces with improvements in their environment, in their instruction and in their training, in their regular work and in their daily living conditions, we prioritize the support of staff so that they can devote with the least number of concerns to their task, which is the protection of our country and, of course, we try to make the best equipment available with the cutting-edge technology available. We seek their safety and also that of all citizens and, at the same time, we strengthen a strategic economic sector such as that of the defence industry in our country. We are talking about a sector, ladies and gentlemen, which exports 80% of its production, which exceeds EUR 5 billion, which maintains 22 000 high-skilled direct jobs, in addition to the thousands of indirect jobs it creates, and which generates EUR 2.5 in the economy for each euro we invest in. These are some of the items that, in my view, are more worth highlighting because they express very clearly the political will of this Government and the management will of the Ministry of Defence with regard to the priority areas of its action, beyond the fact that the budget increase reaches to a greater or lesser extent all the spending policies of my department. And we do not deceive ourselves, this Government is acting against an unquestionable reality, as it is that the defence budget had suffered the economic crisis with particular force. The adjustment did not affect the operation of our Armed Forces because reforms and efficiency programmes were made and the operation was maintained, but it was essential to reverse the trend because there were situations that had already become unsustainable and that still make us worry about preventing the loss of capabilities of our armies, which are directly related to our greater or lesser capacity for defence and protection. In addition, there is a European consensus for the gradual increase of defence budgets in the area of the European Security and Defence Policy, the Fisheries Policy, as well as in the area of the members of the Atlantic Alliance, and in these consensuss Spain is, and it is because we are certain that, given the budgetary situation of our Armed Forces, given the risks and threats present in the current geostrategic scenario, given the part of the burden that we have to assume in the response to this situation of global instability, which exceeds the cyclical situation in order to almost become structural, the only rational position is to invest in our defence and security, in our protection within and outside our borders; to do so head-on, with a view placed in the medium and in the long term, but with solvency, with continuity and with a vocation of permanence. These, in my view, are the guiding principles that must guide any approach we can take towards the establishment of a great agreement on the allocation of defence policy. This is a truncated idea that this Government, despite the complicated political and parliamentary scene, has not abandoned at all. But in the meantime we must continue to work, and we must do so in order to improve the economic possibilities of our defence. With the budget we bring here that a month ago was extensively detailed by my department's senior officials, we are taking a step further in the right direction; a step further in the firm commitment that we will continue to walk the path of growth and security, the path to a strong and committed European Union with security and defence and to work hand in hand with the Atlantic Alliance. This is our purpose and - I am sure - it is also that of the vast majority of Spaniards. Thank you very much, Madam President. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u81	Good morning. Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear in this House to present to you the budgets of the Ministry of Development for the current year 2018, budgets that have been prepared in line with the government's economic policy, which has as its priority objectives to maintain job creation, consolidate and support economic growth and meet public deficit objectives. [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u82	Quiet, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u83	Well, the Budgets of Promotion for the year 2018 take advantage of this favorable context and align with the priority objectives that are already contained in the draft Law of General Budgets of the State for the year 2018. No one will disagree in this House, I am sure, that infrastructure, transport and housing are three fundamental pillars for the modernization, territorial cohesion and the development, both social and economic, of the whole country. Following these initial considerations, I shall go on to detail the budget, which has as its main objectives, first of all, the creation of employment and economic growth by increasing investment in infrastructure; secondly, to fulfil the commitments to infrastructure, transport and housing that we have with the different autonomous communities; thirdly, to improve the quality of infrastructure and transport as well as the mobility of citizens, contributing to territorial vertebration; fourthly, to advance and finalize the major projects that we have under way; fifthly, to increase the productivity of sectors that are particularly involved in the transport of passengers and goods; and finally, to bet on innovation, internationalisation and sustainability. I would like to highlight that the 2018 budgets of the Fomento group provide sufficient budgetary availability to meet the planned actions, and also to meet the main plans that we have recently approved, such as the State Housing Plan 2018-2021, the innovation plan, the proximity plans, the Plan of the Impulse of the Transport of Goods by Rail or the Plan of Investments of Maritime-Terrestrial Accessibility 2017-2021, among others. The consolidated budget of the Fomento group for 2018 amounts to 17 466 million euros, 943 million euros additional to 2017. It is therefore a budget that shows an important advance, clearly bet for the investment, which reaches almost 9 billion euros, because it increases by 17% compared to the budgets of 2017, and because it amounts to 1292 million euros additional, which reflects the government's decided commitment in 2018 for the productive investment to become an engine of economic development. These investments are estimated to generate 235 000 direct and indirect jobs in the Spanish economy, of which some 35 000 will be new jobs. I turn now to the details of the department's investments and its breakdown by sector. The first notable note is that the budget makes a clear investment bet on railway policy, which remains very prominent over the others, totaling 48.1% of investments, with 4286 million euros, with an increase of 25.2% compared to 2017. In order of participation the policies of roads, with 27.3% more, 2431 million euros; ports and maritime security, 10.1% more, with 900 million euros; airports and air security, with 8.4% more, 752 million euros, and housing and soil, with 5.2% more, 467 million euros. In addition, these investments are complemented in an extra-budgetary way by other plans, such as the extraordinary road investment plan or private investment in Spanish ports, which I will also mention throughout my intervention. I will set out below the most significant data on the distribution of the budget by sector and mode of transport. On railways, as I have already mentioned, we will allocate €4286 million, most of the investments. In the railway sector, the budget is a true reflection of the commitments and strategic lines set by the Ministry in the areas of network planning, logistics development, passenger and freight mobility and intermodality. In passenger transport we will continue to work on extending the benefits of high speed to new territories, with the aim of advancing on strategic axes of our network, without leaving aside the conventional network, in which we are already working with the corresponding plans of proximity, and in actions necessary for the replacement of assets, as well as for the integration of the ports in the railway system. In the most immediate horizon, connections such as the tunnel between Atocha and Chamartín, the Vandellós-Tarragona section of the Mediterranean corridor or the Antequera-Granada line are expected to be put into service, sections in which the works have already been completed and in which we are in the testing period. Work is also being done to complete the works at the high speed of the arrival in Murcia, the connection Sale of Baths-Burgos or the Zamoran section of the Galician corridor. In the transport of goods the objective is the establishment of a sustainable system from both economic and environmental point of view and for this it is necessary to achieve an improvement in the efficiency of the railway mode that allows to increase - this is one of the challenges that we have for the year 2018 - the share of participation in the inland transport of goods. These measures will complement the actions on railway infrastructure already under way. For example, our commitment to the Mediterranean and Atlantic corridor, which are fundamental in the international transport of goods, and, for example, the measures supported in these budgets to move forward with the freight plan we have presented. For the year 2018 will be destined to the Mediterranean corridor 923 million euros, which means an increase of 23.4% regarding 2017, and there are endowments for other actions that will enhance this corridor, such as the access to the ports of Barcelona, Castellón, Sagunto, darsena de Escombreras and several terminals. In relation to another important corridor, we have allocated EUR 757 million to continue to make progress in the work of the connection with Galicia. For its part, 661 million euros to connections with Asturias, Cantabria, Basque Country, cantabrian-Mediterranean corridor, with an increase of 26.3% regarding 2017. Finally, the Extremadura corridor is allocated 283 million euros, representing 9.1% more than in 2017. Another of the priority and strategic elements in railway policy are proximity, services that use 424 million users, a fundamental service and vertebrator of metropolitan transport. We will continue throughout this year working intensively to maintain the high level of quality of this service and optimize the provision of services with the aim of capturing a greater number of travelers, including sufficient endowment for the plans already presented for rolling stock and other close proximity plans such as those of Asturias, Cantabria, Valencian Community and Madrid. In 2018 we will allocate to this end 576 million euros, which is 54% more than in 2017. In addition, we will continue, of course, making a bet on the conventional network. ADIF and ADIF High Speed will carry out much of these actions that I have indicated to you, with a total investment volume of 3561 million euros. Well, the conventional network carries 848 million euros, which is 63.1% more than in 2017. I think it is clear that the Spanish Government is committed to improving the conventional network, compatible, of course, with the need to work on high-speed corridors. Finally, investment in Renfe will amount to EUR 378 million, of which 282 will be for mobile equipment. I would also like to highlight at this point the good health of the company, Renfe, which has already achieved a pre-tax profit forecast of around 65 million euros, a benefit based on precise healthy growth that allows its sustainability over time. With regard to roads, we intend to promote the modernization of State roads and ensure their proper maintenance. In this way, the total investment in roads amounts to 2431 million euros, which is 12% more than in 2017. Of these, the amount earmarked for investments in new construction amounts to EUR 1161 million and EUR 917 million for road conservation and safety. In the section of new road infrastructures the budget, as it could not otherwise be, gives priority to the completion of the works that affect the main corridors of great capacity that provide long-distance service between autonomous communities and also to the steps in the border posts, ports and airports of general interest, as corresponds to our competences. The capacity and quality of the network will also be increased by increasing the number of lanes. [[Rumors]]

Finally, we have the public service obligations, in which we must highlight the compensation to the railway services that is made to Renfe for an amount of 1190 million euros, with which we finance 65% of the cost of the tickets declared public service obligation, which is what makes it possible to maintain certain services for the mobility of citizens. As you know, last December the Council of Ministers approved a new declaration of PSOs in railway services and in June we plan to formalise the contract between Renfe and the General Administration of the State for the start-up of these new PSOs. They will have to be added to other air transport PSOs, which are already established and new ones, such as Melilla's connections with some peninsular destinations that are not now served, and the maritime lines of public interest, for which a total of EUR 7.4 million is allocated. As far as the air sector is concerned, also of great importance for economic activity - especially for the tourism sector - and a key element, on the other hand, for the cohesion and vertebration of our territory, we will allocate 752 million euros, an increase of 22.2% over last year. A growth in the context of an 11% fall in fares for the period 2017-2021 and a decrease in air navigation rates according to Enaire's strategic plan, which for 2018 is 3% but which for 2019 will already be 12%, which will make us have the most competitive fare of the main air providers. Enaire has budgeted 86 million for this year, mainly aimed at modernising air navigation systems in line with its 2020 Flight Plan to also meet its European commitments and in particular the construction of the Single European Sky, which will ensure favourable internationalisation and better competition from our industry. Actions will focus on evolving systems, improving efficiency, security and maintaining environmental commitments. For its part, AENA will include the items necessary to carry out the investments in order to be able to adapt to an increase in air traffic that is continuous and that will continue to be so throughout 2018. It is and will continue to be the world's first airport manager and for this we include a €645 million item, which will increase its capacity. In the harbour sector the investment reaches 900 million euros. Ports of the State invests 803 million euros to adapt the harbour infrastructures to the new requirements of the demand, especially associated to the current trends as is the case of the megaships; for the improvement of the operating conditions and security, and for the implementation of new technologies. One of the most important lines, as you already know, within the investment in the port sector is to promote better road and rail accessibility to the Spanish ports, thus improving the competitive position. To this end, EUR 192 million is allocated for access within the Maritime-Terrestrial Accessibility Financial Fund for 2018. To this must be added private investment, which this year is quantified at some EUR 1143 million. Finally, focusing on housing, which is one of the priority lines for the Ministry of Development, is considered four basic pillars. The first of these, as it could not otherwise be, is the Housing Plan that we have already approved, which will enhance the change in housing policy model and focus on facilitating access to housing, the creation of a rental housing park, the promotion of urban and rural rehabilitation, regeneration and renewal, and the improvement of energy efficiency and accessibility. We must also add to this the policy of legislative and regulatory improvement, including the adoption of the Spanish urban agenda, which we will try to present in a further step at the sectoral conference that we will hold at the end of this month, fulfilling the commitments we have as a country in both the European urban agenda and the United Nations urban agenda. We will also invest in the conservation of the Spanish architectural heritage to give continuity to the commitment to recovery and enrichment. The fourth pillar to which I referred is also related to 1.5% cultural, since, although not included in the budgets as such, we launched last year a first call of EUR 50 million and on 11 May we approved in the Council of Ministers a new call that will come out in very few weeks for a very similar amount, another EUR 51 million. In total, the investments collected in housing and architecture in the 2018 budgets, without considering 1.5% cultural, amount to EUR 467 million, of which 426 are related to the State Housing Plan, of which 351 are State rent and rehabilitation aid and 76 are intended for the maintenance of loan subsidy aid. And then there are the specific conventions for the rehabilitation and regeneration of neighbourhoods, which amount to 16.3 million euros, and the 20.6 million that we allocate directly in chapter 6 to architectural rehabilitation. In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, with this review of the content of the budget of the Ministry of Development I try to show that we are facing budgets that are consistent with the economic policy of the Government for the coming years, that are committed to the productive investment of infrastructure, transport and housing, and that meet the commitments of all the territories, in line with the economic moment of recovery that our country is experiencing and that will certainly contribute to the creation of employment. Because we don't have an AVE; we take the planes to go to the doctor, to go to study and it can't be more expensive to travel between Lanzarote and Madrid than between Lanzarote and London and it costs three times as much to come to Madrid. Because our goods have to compete with Moroccans. Because the European Commission told France, Portugal and Spain that the unemployment rates in the outermost regions required special employment plans and as long as we have those unemployment rates we must have them. And, above all, because it could not be that in the autonomous financing system of this country, 400 or 500 million were taken from the Canary Islands every year to compensate for remoteness and insularity, when that money of the financing system had to go to health care, to dependence, to social services. And today those revenues that compensate for remoteness and insularity are not going to compute in the financing system. And next week, when Mr Montoro sits with all the autonomous communities to negotiate the new funding system, the Autonomous Community of the Canary Islands will be on equal footing with the rest of the Spanish. But, most importantly, the agreement with the People's Party will allow this to be anchored in the economic and fiscal regime and in the status of autonomy; that no longer comes a government of the PSOE or the PP, with an absolute majority, that decides that the policies that equalize the canaries with the rest of the Spaniards disappear. That is why the Canary Islands Coalition, by sense of state, because it needs the 200 million for gender-based violence, because we need to raise pensions, because we need investment in this country for employment and because they are fair budgets that need Canary Islands, we are going to support these budgets, because the alternative is worse for this country and because, moreover, the extended budget did so the same party that has presented these budgets. Therefore, in a sense of responsibility and of remembering who put me here with their votes, who is the people of the Canary Islands, Coalition Canaria will support these budgets. Thank you very much, Madam President. [[Applauds]]

If we compare the budget line of the Spanish Royal House with the budget lines of other real houses - we have to remember that the rest of the real houses are also budget lines from the public era - we see that ours, the Spanish Royal House, is the most austere. The British Royal House, 42 million budget; that of Holland, 42.3 million; that of Norway, 18 million; if we look at other systems such as, for example, the French Republic, 113 million. I want to remind you that the Spanish is 7.8 million. If we look, for example, at the Italian Republic, it is 228 million. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, we are talking about a game which, compared to the rest of Europe, is adjusted, moderate and, in addition, a game which has been frozen. It was frozen in 2010, from 2011 to 2014 suffered several reductions, in 2015 and in 2016 it was frozen, it is true that in the 2017 budget it rose by 0.5 and in this it has risen by 0.8. On the website you have detailed everything: the distribution of the budget item, how it is being spent and, of course, with all the expenses, the distribution of all the expenses assumed by the Royal House, subject to all the responsibilities of tax and labour taxes. Ladies and gentlemen - and I finish, Madam President - we are dealing with a part, that of section 01, which is constitutionally binding; it is an austere part, it is a transparent part - there I invite you, I repeat, to enter the website - and it is an absolutely necessary part for your majesty the king to continue to perform the functions entrusted to him by the Constitution. Section 25, Presidency and for Territorial Administrations As you all know, the General Budgets of the State are probably the most important economic and social law presented by a government throughout the year. They represent the fulfilment of the Government's public priorities and define what should be the major State policies. These budgets not only maintain budgetary stability and fulfil our commitments to Europe, but, in particular, continue to drive growth. They are accounts focused on the fact that the recovery that the Spaniards have achieved working all these years also reaches everyone, especially those who made greater efforts throughout the crisis. These are budgets that allow us to promote actions that six or seven years ago we would consider unthinkable in this House, because we must not forget how we were six years ago and how we are now. We were on the verge of rescue, five years in a row of negative growth - it is still a euphemism for the harshness of the situation -, with a deficit of 11% - was in the 9 when we began to draw up the budgets of the year 2011 -, with an unemployment close to 27% and with a destruction of 3.7 million jobs. Well, today we are debating the General State Budgets for 2018, a year in which we have recovered the pre-crisis income level and two thirds of the employment destroyed. Today Spain grows in a strong and balanced way almost for the fifth consecutive year. How did this happen? Budget reform, reform, with an economic and social policy that has fostered stability, something essential in the times ahead, and it is not necessary to look too far to keep in mind. Today, governance management is one of the challenges faced by the most consolidated democracies; and I believe that the adoption of these General State Budgets itself is an example of this. These budgets project good prospects for growth. Therefore, good numbers for the stability of the country, to mark its agenda and to mark the political agenda that citizens need. From the Government we have called for dialogue and responsibility to approve these accounts, to ensure that stability and, therefore, that growth and employment. Because these accounts will be crucial for Spain to continue to create jobs at historical rates and move towards what has to be a country's main goal, achieving 20 million jobs by 2020. Thanks to the dialogue and the agreements shared with trade union organizations that have wanted to sit down and negotiate - and we must thank everyone for the work - we will adopt measures that benefit all citizens. Firstly, to public employees, with an increase in their salaries and salaries resulting from an important agreement reached this same year with the public service unions. For the first time in history, the evolution of the salary of public employees will have a variable part that will depend on the smooth running of the economy and on the fulfilment of our objectives, to compensate for the good doing and good service that our public employees provide, so that they will now be, as at other times they were part of the efforts we made to get out of the crisis, partakers of that growth and return those efforts to them. Secondly, these budgets provide for improvements for pensioners, with increases initially designed to favour lower pensions and which have been extended to all of our pensioners through dialogue and agreement with the rest of the groups. Tax rebates are included for lower incomes, with an impact of EUR 2 billion. Income tax will no longer be paid for those who earn between EUR 12 000 and EUR 14 000; and income will be paid less between EUR 14 000 and EUR 18 000. The draft of these budgets also envisages an increase in autonomous funding, which will allow the autonomous communities, and this is important for the Ministry of Territorial Administration, to receive the largest volume of income in their entire history, 105,020 million euros, to finance services that they provide and that are health, education and social protection; the column, together with pensions, of the welfare state. But these budgets also make it possible to focus on future policies that are on the government's agenda and on the country agenda; policies to address the major global challenges ahead: the digital and technological challenge, the environmental challenge and the demographic challenge and their impact on the sustainability of our welfare state. However, over the past four years - and in line with the gradual recovery of the economy - the National Intelligence Centre has been gradually increasing its budgets, except for the reserved expenditure item, which has remained unchanged since 2013 and which, as you know, is being explained to your ladies and gentlemen in the Commission for this purpose. The increase in this growth path is mainly due to technological renewal. Of the 21 million increases we propose, more than 14 are dedicated to investment projects and the rest to the incorporation and adaptation of staff to technological challenges. The National Intelligence Centre is placing special emphasis on the fight against jihadi terrorism and cyberattacks, noting that in times of crisis the new hybrid threats have affected Spain in order to try to weaken its cohesion and stability. On the other hand, third-party interference is experiencing a growing intensity throughout the European Union and so far unknown, precisely because of the possibilities offered by new technologies. This requires the intelligence service to be ahead, or at least at the level, of those who attack us. Meeting the technological challenge and digital transformation is ultimately a matter of financial availability to make sustained efforts over time. But in addition, investment in technology must be coordinated with the incorporation of the necessary staff to carry it out. The CNI not only needs to increase its workforce quantitatively but, more importantly, to make the qualitative leap that its transformation process implies. This makes it necessary to have staff with new talents and skills, especially in critical areas: computers, engineers, mathematicians, translators and interpreters. It's what you have to live in the age of algorithms. Last year I announced the existence of a multiannual plan to increase the number of members of the centre by 600 people. This plan has allowed new agents to enter the last two exercises and will continue in the coming years. In global terms, the objectives fall within the National Security Strategy 2017 that you know and that gives a comprehensive, effective and coordinated response and a unity of action to the State. Ladies and gentlemen, I shall end by referring to the amendments tabled to section 25. I thank all the groups for the amendments tabled - a total of thirty-one amendments - which, as I said at the beginning of my speech, amount to more than EUR 1 billion. I wouldn't know where to get them; remember we have 441. They will understand that acceptance in its entirety would be, no more or less, to go beyond doubling the ministry's budget. However, there is one issue on which I want to stop because I believe you have been right, you have come to a compromise amendment which, of course, even if it is not necessary at this point, I want you to know that you have the acquiescence and the will to push it forward by this ministry. It refers to the increase in the provision for the EFE Agency's information coverage contract from 12 million to 51 million. We must make an effort and we will do so to provide this body with the necessary resources, which is an engine of Spanish information at a global level and which, as you know, has a new consideration as a service of general economic interest. I'm done. These budgets, as I said at the beginning of my speech, clearly support economic growth and job creation, distribute the benefits of recovery among society, improve the incomes of more than 13 million pensioners, public employees and wage earners and strengthen the provision of basic services with 4248 million more funding that the autonomous communities will receive, so the work of our ministry will be very important. These are budgets in which social expenditure reaches 56 per cent. These are budgets that, if they go forward, will have been for a very important effort of dialogue and understanding among many political groups. All of you, in general, whatever the meaning of your vote, must be thanked for the work you have done and for the cooperation with the Courts - it is up to me to do so - and those who decide to support them will certainly contribute to the sustainable, balanced and distributive growth of our country. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-22-CD180522.u176	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear in this House to present sections 19 and 60, as well as the articulation of the draft General State Budgets for 2018. These are budgets that will undoubtedly serve to consolidate that social recovery that Spanish society is experiencing today. We are facing the most social budgets of our history, with social spending as never before; budgets that devote 56% of the expenditure to social items, which is 40% more than Spain invested in social spending in 2007, for giving them a figure. They are budgets that move forward and leave no one behind in this recovery and that will also help us to achieve at the end of next year that great national goal, and that is that Spain will get more than 20 million people working, as there were before the crisis, which is the same as saying that the recovery reaches all Spaniards. Thanks to Spanish society we are making progress, creating more jobs - half a million jobs in recent years - and these budgets will consolidate those almost 500,000 jobs in 2018. That is why Spain today leads the job creation in the European Union and has managed, since 2014, to recover 75% of all jobs destroyed in the crisis. These are budgets that also advocate better jobs. We know that we have a long way to go, but there is no doubt that stability and improvement in the quality of employment remain a reality month by month with these budgets. We already accumulated, with the month of April, fifty-one months of consecutive increase in indefinite recruitment in Spain, and with one million less occupied in our country, we already have the level of indefinite employment prior to the crisis. Thanks to Spanish society, employment is being created throughout the national territory, in all the autonomous communities, in all economic sectors and also in those groups that found it more difficult to integrate into the labour market. That is what we call moving forward without leaving anyone behind, and I am referring in particular to young people, among whom employment is growing twice as much as the national average today; women, because today we have more members of Social Security than ever before in our country - 8,660,000; self-employed people, among whom intense employment is also created, because self-employment is now a source of opportunity; people with disabilities, among whom employment is also at its peak. And, of course, it is also good news to know that last year nine out of ten people who left unemployment in our country were long-term unemployed, because those opportunities are reaching the people, the families who need it the most. As I said, there is a lot of work to be done, because, although we have recovered 75% of the jobs destroyed by the crisis, we have to find opportunities for 3 300 000 people who remain on the unemployment lists, people who deserve to work, who want to work and who we are all going to help to find that opportunity as soon as possible. Let us continue to add opportunities with these General State Budgets for 2018. With them we will get Spain Section 19, Employment and Social Security, and Section 60, Social Security each day continue to generate more and better jobs, which improves the lives of those who are among pensioners, with increases in all pensions and in particular lower pensions and widowhood pensions. These budgets also support political stability, which is the best way to continue to grow in a solid and social way. The citizens gave us a mandate and a clear message that meant we agreed, and these budgets are also the budgets of the agreement and the pact. If the negotiations that we have conducted from the Government, led by the Vice-President and the President of the Government, in recent times culminate with the support of seven parliamentary groups, we will be making possible that constructive dialogue, that sum of efforts, so that Spain not only has political stability, but also completes that economic and social recovery that Spanish society demands of us. Ladies and gentlemen, I am now entering the debate on sections 19 and 60 of the budget. Section 19 is devoted to employment policies. The budget envisages EUR 24 552 million through the Secretariat of State for Employment to continue to advance with priorities: to strengthen the pace of job creation, guaranteeing, as I said, the integration of workers without leaving anyone behind; to improve the protection of workers who remain unemployed; to activate them so that the time back to the labour market is as minimum as possible and to accompany our entire workforce, this talent and human capital, which is the great treasure of Spanish society, so that it adapts to the new transformations that the economy, the digital economy, globalisation and the challenges and challenges that we face. Active policies are undoubtedly that great tool of integration for workers who are in unemployment today. Today, investment in active policies by unemployed people increases by 10% compared to last year and is close to 2000 euros per unemployed person in 2018. It is undoubtedly a commitment to investment in human capital, which is Spain's great strategic asset. These budgets also implement the Spanish Employment Activation Strategy 2017-2020, agreed with the autonomous communities and the social partners. It is a strategy in which we want to make further progress in modernising and coordinating public employment services in order to improve the effectiveness and efficiency of active policies and so that the new tools we are using, such as statistical profiling, the single employment portal, the training account or big data and new technologies help us simplify the return time to the labour market of the unemployed in our country. We also want to make a special effort in those groups that have had it most difficult in the crisis, for example young people, the long-term unemployed and those who continue to have it difficult and are at risk of social exclusion. We want to make a special effort in training, which is the great bridge to the labour market, particularly in dual vocational training, by increasing the participation of the autonomous communities in vocational training for employment and by improving the coordination of all public administrations. These budgets, ladies and gentlemen, devote EUR 3 billion to training, and I would like to highlight in particular the efforts made for training among young people. If we have almost a million young people in unemployment today, the reality is that half of them have not completed compulsory secondary education. Therefore, training them quickly, which acquire new skills, new capacities to meet the demands of the productive fabric is essential for their employability and for their return to the labour market. We will make an exceptional effort in these budgets to launch, within the framework of the Ministry of Employment, dual vocational training with two clear initiatives. The first is the Youth Guarantee training bonus, which will allow a young person under thirty years of age to train and work at the same time with an additional aid of EUR 430 from the Youth Guarantee, so that he will receive for eighteen months for work and training, through this training and learning contract, a reward of more than EUR 1000, which is certainly a guarantee to encourage entrepreneurs and young people to do this standard of employment and training for our younger workers. We want that job in training to become stable; we want a quality job and we are going to improve the conversion of that training and learning contract into an indefinite contract, giving entrepreneurs to make a €3,000 bonus for three years to give stability to that young person who is born into employment with a training contract. These are budgets, ladies and gentlemen, which also agree on self-employment. The autonomous were undoubtedly the first protagonists of recovery in Spain; today the implementation of the Law on Urgent Reforms of Self-Employment, which you unanimously agreed in this House with the Government, is yielding results. This year's budgets include measures to increase support for entrepreneurship, to improve the social protection of the whole group of self-employed persons and, in particular, to reconcile and improve self-employed women. There are the flat rate of 50 euros, which is increased to one year, the contribution only for the periods actually worked and, as I said to them, the support for conciliation and the motherhood of the autonomous woman as major protagonists in these budgets. These are measures that are beginning to work. In just four months there are more than 40,000 new self-employed registered in the RETA, undoubtedly a collective success of this whole House in supporting self-employment, which is a cornerstone of employment in Spain. In these budgets, moreover - and I would like to thank the work done by the Vice-President of the Government and the Commissioner for Demography and Depopulation in Spain - a flat-rate rural rate -50 euros for two years - is incorporated by amendment for those entrepreneurs who live in municipalities with less than 5000 inhabitants, in order to promote entrepreneurship and also to ensure that the employment and opportunities of Spanish people continue to be facilitated in all parts of Spain. These are concrete measures in this budget to promote employment and entrepreneurship throughout the national territory. In addition, the employment promotion programme has a budget of EUR 1444 million and incorporates important programmes, such as joint action to improve the care of long-term unemployed persons, in coordination with the autonomous communities; the agricultural employment promotion programme, which is a key element for the rural and Andalusian world; and also those employment and training programmes for persons with disabilities in special employment centres, which achieve the integration of the most vulnerable groups in our society. Ladies and gentlemen, I have referred to the first priority, continuing to create more jobs and accelerating job creation in Spain, which is the great national objective. The second is that we continue to create jobs without leaving anyone behind. This is why these budgets maintain and improve the protection of workers who are still unemployed and who are looking for a job opportunity. The State Employment Public Service devotes 7 out of 10 euros of its resources to unemployment protection. It is a similar investment, ladies and gentlemen, to last year, despite the sharp reduction in unemployment in the last year. This means that it improves coverage by at least two points compared to the previous year. Undoubtedly, this Government is committed to the people in need of greater protection and has launched - by dialogue with the social partners and also with the autonomous communities within the framework of the sectoral conference - a new initiative to maintain and improve unemployment protection in Spain: the supplementary income of unemployment, what we call the RED programme, which is in the process of constructive dialogue, I insist, with the social partners and autonomous communities. This will allow Spain to have a higher level of unemployment protection in 2018 than before the crisis and will allow us to make stable the temporary protection that existed in programs such as the Preparation or the RAI or the Stop Activation Program. In addition, it will allow such protection to reach, with seventy-two months of coverage, those most in need of protection, such as long-term unemployed and over forty-five years of family responsibilities. In this constructive dialogue with the autonomous communities and with the social partners, we continue, ladies and gentlemen, and in the meantime this budget receives an extraordinary unemployment subsidy, in effect until the unemployment rate drops by 15%, so that there is no loss of coverage because the Prep and PAE programmes are already completed, depending on the circumstances, because they were temporary programmes. Ladies and gentlemen, I encourage you to join this constructive debate to improve the social protection of the unemployed in Spain. Ladies and gentlemen, at this point I would also like to refer to another initiative that is reflected in the General Budgets of the State: the universal social card, which was promoted by the Vice-President of the Government together with President Rajoy at the January 2017 Conference of Presidents. It is an initiative that has been dialogued and agreed with the Autonomous Communities, the social partners and also with the EMFF. It is certainly a great tool to improve the care of all people in Spain. It is a universal social card that facilitates transparency and, administratively, simplifies the burden on the citizen and favours preferential care for those who need special care. In addition, it has a universal character. Why? Because this card is constituted as an accreditation mechanism, opening the door to all public policies, but also to all social policies that civil society wants to do within the framework of this policy of corporate social responsibility. It is therefore a mechanism of coordination, transparency, attention to the people who need it most, but also a universal card that confirms our particular situation in favour of the social policies of public administrations and also of civil society. Ladies and gentlemen, this budget addresses the important, present and future of the labour market in Spain, which coincides with what we call in the Ministry of Employment the talent agenda, which means not only that we activate all workers - regardless of age, gender or their training - but also that we have these workers permanently adapted to the challenges and challenges of the time we live in, such as globalisation, revolution 4.0, digitisation and even that demographic challenge that we have on the horizon. This improvement, without doubt, also bears the seal of these budgets, with the State plan for training in digital skills, the first plan that is made of these characteristics in Spain, with 60 million euros, born of social dialogue and will allow to adapt our workers to the new digital economy, source of opportunities for the present and for the future of Spain. This budget also contains, and I want to make it clear, improvements in working conditions. Not only do we want more and better jobs, but with this budget we also want to give a boost to the highest wages in Spain: higher wages in the field of public service and improvements in lower wages, with the rise of the interprofessional minimum wage that we have agreed with the social partners. These are certainly two important signs that, within the framework of the bipartisan dialogue, trade unions and employers will also work together and help from their responsibility and with commitment to ensure that the recovery in Spain will also be accompanied by higher wages in the present and in the future. We are also working on these better working conditions from the Labour and Social Security Inspectorate, which is, as you know, the last guarantor of the quality of employment in Spain. We reinforce in these budgets the most important asset of the inspection, which is the 3,000 people between inspectors, subcontractors and support staff, in order to promote the quality of employment in Spain. In addition, the State Labour Inspection and Social Security Agency and the National Anti-Fraud Office are operational in the Strategic Inspection Plan, which will improve the effectiveness of this fight against unemployment, irregular employment and social security fraud. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, I refer to Section 60 of these budgets, Social Security. These budgets reflect the Government's commitment to all pensioners: to make it possible to live a recovery by paying more pensions to more pensioners and higher than ever in our country. It is a social expenditure that is maintained as the main priority and its exponent is re-expenditure on pensions, which means 41% of the State budget. The one dedicated to pensions is, by contrast, the fundamental part of the Spanish budget. Social Security and Passive Classes represent a total expenditure of EUR 146,300 million, because the budget provides for the increase of all pensions, both contributory and non-contributory. In these 2018 budgets all pensions are raised again, generally 1.6%; minimum and non-contributory pensions are raised 3% and widow's pensions are raised 7.6%. It is about returning the effort that our elders have made in the exit from the crisis and in the beginning of the recovery. [[Rumors]]
